# PART I — STATUS, MANDATE, AND THE BOUNDARY OF THIS INSTRUMENT

## I.1 The absolute status rule

**[P]** Mandate §1. **The rule is reproduced here because every finding in this instrument is subject to it, and because a research instrument is the document most likely to be mistaken for an opinion.**

| # | **This instrument must never be represented as** | **What it is instead** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Legal advice** | **A record of what publicly available authoritative material appears to say, on the research date, with its conditions and its limits** |
| **2** | **A lawyer's opinion** | **No lawyer has read the architecture. **Every finding is tagged and classified, and `PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REQUIRED` appears wherever an opinion is needed** |
| **3** | **A chartered accountant's opinion** | **As above, for accounting and tax** |
| **4** | **A tax opinion** | **No tax position is taken. **Where a rate or threshold is quoted it is quoted with its source, its date and its verification status** |
| **5** | **Professional certification** | **Nothing here certifies anything** |
| **6** | **Regulatory approval** | **No regulator has been approached, and no application has been made** |
| **7** | **Permission to operate** | **`C-25` and `GATE P0` = 0 of 10 stand exactly where `ID-1` left them. **§XXIV item 6 records what must remain stopped** |
| **8** | **Confirmation that the organisation is legally compliant** | **There is no organisation. **`FA-4`, `PP-03`, and the CEA's finding that *"there is no legal person to incur cost, hold property or be sued"* are unchanged** |

**The permitted status, used for this instrument as a whole and available for use against any single finding:**

> ### **`RESEARCH-BASED PRE-VALIDATION — PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION STILL REQUIRED WHERE IDENTIFIED`**

## I.2 What changed since `ID-1`, and why research was the right next act

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — `ID-1` §XXX.2 and §XXX.3 item 9; **`U-PD-16`**; **[S-K(E): §XXVI.3]**.

| Item | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **What `ID-1` concluded** | **That the highest-value next human action is to copy §XXIII.1, complete its sender fields, sign it and send it to a qualified Indian corporate and tax counsel, paying personally if necessary** |
| **Why that action has not been performed** | **The founder does not currently have access to paid legal counsel. **`BLK-D-07` records that the professional fees required are *"not stated anywhere"*; **CEA `F-10`** records an unfunded mandate; **`C-15`** and **`PP-04`** mean the organisation cannot pay** |
| **The two wrong responses to that fact** | **One is to wait indefinitely, which leaves the architecture frozen with nothing recorded against the wait. **The other is to invent legal conclusions to fill the gap, which **`PP-01 … PP-09`**, **`C-26`** and mandate §21 of this task all forbid** |
| **The response this instrument takes** | **Research the questions against public authoritative material, classify each result honestly, preserve every uncertainty, and reduce the future cost of professional validation. **Mandate §24: *"do not conclude that Sewak Sathi may launch"*. **§XXIV item 5 records what may safely continue** |
| **What this instrument does not do to `ID-1`** | **It does not supersede, amend, renumber, correct or replace `ID-1`. **It answers, in part and provisionally, questions that `ID-1` registered. **All thirty-five `CQ-` questions remain open; **§XX records for each what research did and did not establish** |
| **What this instrument does not do to the CEA** | **It does not rewrite the CEA. **Mandate §25 lists the findings that must remain intact, and **§XVIII records every architectural consequence as one of four permitted effects, never as an amendment** |

## I.3 The boundary — what research may and may not do here

**[P]** Mandate §§21, 22, 24, 25. **Each row states a boundary and the device that keeps this instrument inside it.**

| # | **The boundary** | **The device** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Research may establish what a source says. It may not establish what a source means for this organisation** | **Every finding carries two mandatory fields — *"what the source actually establishes"* and *"what the source does not establish"* — and the second is never left empty** |
| **2** | **Research may narrow a question. It may not answer a question that turns on facts nobody has supplied** | **The `FACT-DEPENDENT` classification, and §XXII's list of the missing facts. **`ID-1`'s disclosure pack remains blank and is not filled by inference** |
| **3** | **Research may compare entities. It may not select one** | **Mandate §5. **The word **`ENTITY-DEPENDENT`** is used until the structure is actually selected, and **Part VIII** is a matrix with every cell marked `SOURCE`, `CONDITION` or `UNKNOWN`** |
| **4** | **Research may quote a rate or threshold that has been verified. It may not quote one that has not** | **Every figure carries its source, its date and one of four tags: **`VERIFIED — PRIMARY`**, **`VERIFIED — SECONDARY`**, **`SOURCE CONFLICT`**, **`CURRENT-LAW PROFESSIONAL VERIFICATION REQUIRED`**. **No figure appears without one** |
| **5** | **Research may identify a conflict between sources. It may not resolve it** | **Mandate §20 and **Part XVII**: source A, source B, dates, hierarchy where established, the apparent conflict, whether it can be reconciled, and whether professional interpretation is required — then **`SOURCE CONFLICT — PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REQUIRED`**** |
| **6** | **Research may clarify a legal constraint around a CEA finding. It may not alter the finding** | **Mandate §25 and **Part XVIII**: the four permitted effects are `NO ARCHITECTURAL CHANGE`, `CLARIFICATION REQUIRED`, `ARCHITECTURAL AMENDMENT MAY BE REQUIRED` and `BLOCKED PENDING PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`. **None of them is "amended"** |
| **7** | **Research may say a proposition appears supported. It may not use the prohibited formulations** | **Mandate §21. **The eleven prohibited phrases are listed at §II.4 with the permitted substitutes, and §XXIII's `RA-13` tests the whole file against them** |
| **8** | **Research may reduce uncertainty. It may not eliminate it** | **§XXII states what public research cannot establish, and **§XIX** keeps the professional-resolved register open** |

## I.4 The five prohibited conversions, carried forward unchanged

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — `ID-1` §XXVII.3; **`PP-01 … PP-09`**; **Rule K-4**; the five forbidden assumptions of `v1.3-H`. **Research does not relax any of these. A source that appears to describe Krytos as a parent does not make it one.**

| # | **The conversion that remains forbidden** | **Why research does not change it** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | ***"Owns and controls"* → incorporated ownership** | **Research establishes what Indian law requires of ownership. **It cannot establish who owns anything here, because no document of ownership exists — **`OC-10`: 0 of 17 asset classes have a stated owner**** |
| **2** | ***"India jurisdiction"* → completed registration** | **Part VIII establishes what registration each candidate form requires. **It does not register anything, and **`FA-3`** remains an asserted jurisdiction** |
| **3** | ***"Ecosystem head"* → legally recognised parent** | **No source located in this research confers parent status on a body that does not exist. **`UE-24`** continues to block **`OPT-I`**** |
| **4** | ***"Virtual Brain"* → legal person** | **Every legal-person test located in this research requires a constitutive act — incorporation, registration, a deed, a licence. **None has occurred** |
| **5** | ***"Founder"* → legally recognised shareholder** | **Shareholding requires a company and a subscription. **Neither exists. **`CF-13`'s verification — *"no invented ownership — VERIFIED"* — still holds** |

# PART II — SOURCE HIERARCHY, CLASSIFICATION, AND THE METHOD

## II.1 The source hierarchy, applied

**[P]** Mandate §2. **Three tiers. Every source in Part XXI carries a tier, and no Tier 3 source is used as authoritative proof where Tier 1 or Tier 2 material was available.**

| Tier | **What it is** | **What it may be used for here** | **What it may not be used for** |
|---|---|---|---|
| ### **`TIER 1`** | **Primary and official Indian sources — India Code and official Acts; the Ministry of Corporate Affairs; the Income Tax Department; CBIC and the GST Council; the Department of Revenue; the Ministry of Labour and Employment; EPFO and ESIC; the RBI; official notifications; official rules and regulations; official court and judicial sources; official standard-setting sources. **The version applicable as at the research date** | **Establishing what the law says, what a notification requires, what a regulator directs, and what a court held** | **Establishing what the law means for this organisation, which requires facts and professional judgement** |
| ### **`TIER 2`** | **Authoritative professional material — ICAI; recognised professional institutions; established legal and tax research publications; high-quality professional commentary. **Always labelled secondary** | **Corroborating Tier 1, supplying the current administrative position where Tier 1 is not published in accessible form, and locating primary sources** | **Substituting for Tier 1 where Tier 1 exists. **A Tier 2 statement of a rate or threshold is marked `VERIFIED — SECONDARY` and never `VERIFIED — PRIMARY`** |
| ### **`TIER 3`** | **Secondary commentary — blogs, articles, explainers, forums** | **Locating relevant issues, discovering terminology, and identifying primary sources** | **Authoritative proof of any proposition where primary material is available. **Where Tier 3 is the only material located, the finding is classified **`SUPPORTED BY SECONDARY SOURCE ONLY`** or **`NOT ESTABLISHED`**, never **`SUPPORTED BY PRIMARY SOURCE`**** |

## II.2 The seven-step method, and what was actually done at each step

**[P]** Mandate §6. **Each step is recorded here with the discipline applied, so that a reader can audit the method and not merely the conclusion.**

| Step | **What the method requires** | **How it was performed in this research** | **Where the result appears** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1 Identify the exact question** | **Do not broaden it** | **Each finding begins from an `ID-1` identifier — a `CQ-` question, a `PVR-` item, a `TX-` or `AH-` head, an `SR-` register entry — and is narrowed to the single proposition the source can bear** | **Field 3 of every finding** |
| **2 Identify the legal domain** | **Company law, partnership and LLP law, labour law, contract law, tax, GST, accounting, banking, data and privacy, insurance, liability, safeguarding** | **Named per finding, using the domain list in the mandate rather than a generic label** | **Field 4** |
| **3 Find primary sources** | **The actual Act, Rule, Regulation, notification, government material or judgment** | **Tier 1 was sought first for every finding. **Where it was located — the RBI KYC Directions, the India Code text of the Code on Wages and of the Indian Trusts Act, the Income Tax Department's own guidance page, PIB — it is cited as Tier 1. **Where it was not, the highest available tier is cited and the gap is recorded** | **Field 6, and **Part XXI**** |
| **4 Verify current status** | **Check amendments and effective dates** | **This is the step that produced the most consequential results of the whole exercise, because three of the statutes the architecture would have to be built against changed between 2024 and 2026** | **Field 7, and **Part III**** |
| **5 Identify conditions** | **Never quote a rule without its conditions** | **Field 8 of every finding is mandatory and is never *"none"*. **A rule with no stated conditions is a rule that has been quoted badly** | **Field 8** |
| **6 Map to Sewak Sathi** | **Say exactly which architectural question the source affects** | **Field 2 carries the architectural identifier, and field 13 carries the consequence** | **Fields 2 and 13** |
| **7 Classify the result** | **One of nine classifications** | **§II.3. **Every finding carries exactly one, and the classification is derived from the evidence and not from the usefulness of the answer** | **Field 14** |

## II.3 The nine classifications

**[P]** Mandate §6, step 7. **These nine are the only permitted classifications. They are not interchangeable, and the test for each is stated so that a finding cannot be upgraded into a stronger one.**

| Classification | **The test — a finding may carry this classification only if** | **What it does not mean** |
|---|---|---|
| ### **`SUPPORTED BY PRIMARY SOURCE`** | **A Tier 1 source was located, its text or official summary was read, and the proposition is what the source says** | **That the proposition applies to Sewak Sathi. **Application is fields 8 and 13** |
| ### **`SUPPORTED BY SECONDARY SOURCE ONLY`** | **Only Tier 2 or Tier 3 material was located, and no Tier 1 source was found for the proposition** | **That the proposition is wrong. **It means it is unverified against the primary text** |
| ### **`PARTIALLY SUPPORTED`** | **The source supports part of the proposition and is silent or unclear on the rest** | **That the unsupported part is false** |
| ### **`ENTITY-DEPENDENT`** | **The answer differs according to which legal form is chosen, and no form has been chosen** | **That the answer is unknowable. **Part VIII shows what each candidate form would produce** |
| ### **`FACT-DEPENDENT`** | **The answer turns on a fact that no source can supply — how people actually work, what has actually happened, who holds what** | **That research failed. **It means research was the wrong instrument for that part** |
| ### **`AMBIGUOUS`** | **The source is capable of more than one reading and this instrument will not choose between them** | **That the ambiguity is resolvable by more research. **Often it is resolvable only by interpretation** |
| ### **`SOURCE CONFLICT`** | **Two or more sources of comparable standing say different things** | **That one is wrong. **Part XVII records both, their dates, the hierarchy where established, and whether the conflict can be reconciled** |
| ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED`** | **No source sufficient to the proposition was located in this research** | **That the proposition is false, or that no source exists anywhere. **It means this research did not find one** |
| ### **`PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | **The proposition cannot responsibly be settled by public research at all — it needs facts, judgement, interpretation, or a filing** | **That the research was wasted. **`ID-1` exists precisely to route these questions** |

## II.4 The prohibited formulations, and the permitted substitutes

**[P]** Mandate §21. **The eleven phrases below do not appear as assertions anywhere in this instrument. Where they appear at all, they appear inside this table, as prohibitions. `RA-13` at Part XXIII tests the whole file against them.**

| # | **Prohibited** | **Permitted substitute used instead** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | ***"This is legal."*** | ***"The available source indicates that, subject to the stated conditions, …"*** |
| **2** | ***"This is illegal."*** | ***"The available source indicates a prohibition, subject to …"*** |
| **3** | ***"The organisation can legally …"*** | ***"If a legal person existed and the stated conditions were met, the source indicates …"*** |
| **4** | ***"The founder can legally …"*** | ***"The source addresses the position of a person in the stated capacity, subject to …"*** |
| **5** | ***"The members are legally protected."*** | ***"The source provides a protection with the stated scope and the stated limits"*** — **and, for **`MV Act s.134A`**, the scope is confined to accidents involving a motor vehicle: **RF-24**** |
| **6** | ***"No liability exists."*** | ***"No source located in this research establishes liability on these facts; the question is `FACT-DEPENDENT`"*** |
| **7** | ***"No registration is required."*** | ***"The source indicates that registration is not required below the stated threshold, and that the threshold is displaced where the stated conditions arise"*** |
| **8** | ***"This structure is compliant."*** | ***"The source states the conditions this structure would have to satisfy"*** |
| **9** | ***"This is tax-free."*** | ***"The source provides an exemption subject to the stated conditions, and the conditions include registration that does not exist"*** |
| **10** | ***"GST does not apply."*** | ***"The source exempts the stated activity, supplied by an entity holding the stated registration, and both conditions are unmet"*** |
| **11** | ***"No labour law applies."*** | ***"The source's application turns on the stated thresholds and the stated classification, neither of which is established"*** |

| Prefix | **What it names** | **Collision check** | **Result** |

| **`SCF-01 …`** | **Source conflicts — Part XVII** | **Searched** | ### **`0 — CLEAN`** |
| **`ACN-01 …`** | **Architectural consequence register — Part XVIII** | **Searched** | ### **`0 — CLEAN`** |
| **`RL-01 …`** | **Research limitations — Part XXII** | **Searched** | ### **`0 — CLEAN`** |
| **`RQ-01 …`** | **Research-resolved questions — Part XIX** | **Searched** | ### **`0 — CLEAN`** |
| **`PRQ-01 …`** | **Professional-resolved questions — Part XIX, which remains open** | **Searched** | ### **`0 — CLEAN`** |
| **Prefixes deliberately not used** | **`PQ-`** — **113 occurrences across `v1.3-G`, `v1.3-H`, `v1.3-I`, `v1.3-J` and `v1.3-K`, where `PQ-01 …` names prohibited acts. **This prefix was drafted into the table above, the collision check was then run, and the prefix was replaced by `PRQ-` before first use — which is the rule operating as intended, and is recorded so that the near-miss is visible.** **`DC-`** — 55 occurrences in this repository (`v1.3-H` `DC-01 … DC-nn`). **`RV-`** — **[S-D:83]** review types. **`RC-`** — **[S-E:1746]**. **`AC-`** — **[S-C:1341]** anti-cult controls and the CEA's accrual rules. **`RS-`** — **[S-E:880]** and CEA `RS-9`. **`M-`** — **[S-C:1078]**. **`LV-`** — **[S-F:160]** and **[S-F:126]**. **`C-`** — **[S-A:2145]** and **[S-K(E):1594]**. **`BR-`** — **[S-E:87]** | — | **Rejected before use** |

# PART III — DATE CONTROL: THE LAW MOVED

## III.1 Why this Part comes first

**[P]** Mandate §3. **This is a time-sensitive legal and tax research exercise, and the first finding of the exercise is that the legal landscape the architecture was written against has changed three times since it was written. Any research that had not begun here would have produced confident answers to questions asked of repealed statutes.**

> ### **`FOUR REGIME CHANGES BEAR DIRECTLY ON THIS ARCHITECTURE. THREE OF THEM POST-DATE THE CORPUS. THE FOURTH — THE DIGITAL PERSONAL DATA PROTECTION ACT — HAS A COMMENCEMENT SCHEDULE THAT PUTS ITS CORE OBLIGATIONS IN THE FUTURE, AND ONE OF ITS PHASES FALLS DUE FIVE WEEKS AFTER THIS RESEARCH DATE.`**

## III.2 The date-control register

**[P]** Every entry records the research date, the publication date, the effective date, the amendment date where one was located, and whether the source appears current. **Where current applicability could not be established, the entry says so in those words.**

| ID | **The regime** | **What changed** | **Effective date** | **Source and tier** | **Current-status assessment** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`DTC-01`** | ### **`Direct tax — the Income-tax Act, 2025`** | **The Income-tax Act, 1961 (Act No. 43 of 1961) is replaced in its entirety by the Income-tax Act, 2025 (Act No. 30 of 2025). **The revised Bill cleared the Lok Sabha on 11 August 2025 and the Rajya Sabha on 12 August 2025; **presidential assent followed on 21 August 2025**; **the Act fixes its own commencement at 1 April 2026. **The structure is reduced from more than 700 sections to **536 sections across 23 chapters with 16 schedules**, and the twin concepts of *previous year* and *assessment year* collapse into a single **Tax Year** beginning 1 April. **The Income Tax Rules, 2026 came into effect on the same date, replacing the Income-tax Rules, 1962** | ### **`1 APRIL 2026`** | **Multiple corroborating secondary sources — **SRC-04**, **SRC-05**, **SRC-06**, **SRC-07**, **SRC-08**; **an ICAI concordance document — **SRC-09**. **Tier 2 and Tier 3. **The primary text of the Act was not obtained in this research** | **`CURRENT — the commencement date is corroborated across five independent sources including an ICAI document. **The primary text is **`NOT OBTAINED`**, so every section number attributed to the 2025 Act in this instrument is **`CURRENT-LAW PROFESSIONAL VERIFICATION REQUIRED`**** |
| **`DTC-02`** | ### **`Which Act governs which income`** | **Income earned up to and including 31 March 2026 is governed by the Income-tax Act, 1961. **Income earned from 1 April 2026 is governed by the Income-tax Act, 2025. **Everything for FY 2025-26, including forms issued by 15 June 2026 and the fourth-quarter return, stayed under the 1961 Act** | **`1 APRIL 2026`** | **SRC-05**, **SRC-06** — **Tier 3** | **`CURRENT`. **This matters to the architecture because any entity constituted now would have its first Tax Year wholly under the 2025 Act** |
| **`DTC-03`** | ### **`Labour law — the four Labour Codes`** | **The Code on Wages, 2019; the Industrial Relations Code, 2020; the Code on Social Security, 2020; and the Occupational Safety, Health and Working Conditions Code, 2020 were made effective, **rationalising 29 existing central labour laws**. **The Code on Wages now applies to all employees irrespective of any wage ceiling, replacing the earlier limit of ₹24,000 per month under the Payment of Wages Act** | ### **`21 NOVEMBER 2025`** | ### **`PIB Delhi, Ministry of Labour & Employment, press release dated 21 November 2025 — SRC-01, PRID 2192463; and PIB release PRID 2193095 dated 23 November 2025 — SRC-02. **`TIER 1`** | ### **`CURRENT — ESTABLISHED BY TIER 1 SOURCE`** |
| **`DTC-04`** | **Labour Codes — the status of the Rules** | **The Codes are in force, but the detailed Central and State Rules required for full implementation were reported as still pending by one source in December 2025 and as notified for all four Codes by another source in 2026, with state-wise roll-out incomplete and six states recorded as having notified final rules** | **Contested** | **SRC-03** against **SRC-10**, **SRC-11**, **SRC-12** — **Tier 2 and Tier 3** | ### **`CURRENT APPLICABILITY NOT ESTABLISHED AS TO THE RULES`** — **recorded as **SCF-02**, not resolved** |
| **`DTC-05`** | ### **`Data protection — the DPDP Act, 2023`** | **The Digital Personal Data Protection Act, 2023 (Act No. 22 of 2023) received assent on 11 August 2023 but was commenced only in phases, by notification **`G.S.R. 843(E)` dated 13 November 2025**, published in the Official Gazette on 14 November 2025. **The Digital Personal Data Protection Rules, 2025 were notified on 13 November 2025 by **`G.S.R. 844(E)`, `845(E)` and `846(E)`**, published 14 November 2025. **Phase 1 (immediate) brought into force s.1(2), s.2 definitions, ss.18 to 26, s.35, ss.38 to 43 and s.44(1) and (3) — establishing the Data Protection Board of India. **Phase 2, one year from publication, brings in s.6(9) and s.27(1)(d). **Phase 3, eighteen months from publication, brings in ss.3 to 5, s.6(1) to (8) and (10), ss.7 to 17, s.27 except 27(1)(d), ss.28 to 34, ss.36 and 37, and s.44(2) — the notice, consent, legitimate-use, children's-data, significant-data-fiduciary, rights, appeals, penalties and blocking provisions. **In the Rules: Rules 1, 2 and 17 to 21 came into force on publication; **Rule 4 one year after; **Rules 3, 5 to 16, 22 and 23 eighteen months after** | **Phase 1: **`13/14 NOVEMBER 2025`**. Phase 2: **`13 NOVEMBER 2026`**. Phase 3: **`13 MAY 2027`**** | **SRC-13 (PwC regulatory insights, 16 November 2025)**, **SRC-14**, **SRC-15**, **SRC-16**, **SRC-17** — **Tier 2 and Tier 3, all corroborating the same gazette numbers and the same three phases** | ### **`CURRENT, AND DECISIVE: AS AT THE RESEARCH DATE OF 8 OCTOBER 2026, THE CORE PROCESSING OBLIGATIONS ARE **NOT YET IN FORCE**. THEY COMMENCE ON OR ABOUT 13 MAY 2027. PHASE 2 FALLS DUE ON OR ABOUT 13 NOVEMBER 2026 — APPROXIMATELY FIVE WEEKS AFTER THIS RESEARCH DATE`** |
| **`DTC-06`** | ### **`Criminal law — the three Sanhitas`** | **The Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, 2023 (Act No. 45 of 2023, 358 sections), the Bharatiya Nagarik Suraksha Sanhita, 2023 and the Bharatiya Sakshya Adhiniyam, 2023 replaced the Indian Penal Code, 1860, the Code of Criminal Procedure, 1973 and the Indian Evidence Act, 1872. **Assent to the BNS was given on 25 December 2023. **One secondary source records that all provisions came into force except s.106(2), concerning death caused by rash or negligent driving, which was held in abeyance** | ### **`1 JULY 2024`** | **SRC-18**, **SRC-19**, **SRC-20** — **Tier 3, corroborating** | **`CURRENT`. **The abeyance of s.106(2) is **`SUPPORTED BY SECONDARY SOURCE ONLY`** and is recorded, not relied on** |
| **`DTC-07`** | **GST — an amendment to the time-of-supply provisions** | **One source records that **s.12(4) of the CGST Act, 2017**, dealing with vouchers, was **deleted with effect from 1 October 2025** | **`1 OCTOBER 2025`** | **SRC-21** — **Tier 3** | ### **`CURRENT APPLICABILITY NOT ESTABLISHED`** — **a single Tier 3 source; **the deletion is not relied on anywhere in this instrument and is recorded so that a professional checks it** |
| **`DTC-08`** | **Accounting standards — the applicability roadmap** | **Ind AS applicability continues under the Companies (Indian Accounting Standards) Rules, 2015, made under s.133 of the Companies Act, 2013: **mandatory for every listed company other than SME-exchange-listed, and for every unlisted company whose net worth reaches ₹250 crore, together with their holding, subsidiary, associate and joint venture companies. **Entities below those thresholds follow the older Accounting Standards under the Companies (Accounting Standards) Rules, 2021. **Ind AS 115 replaced Ind AS 18 for annual periods beginning on or after 1 April 2018. **One source records a December 2025 revision to the *small company* threshold at s.2(85), which is a different test and does not affect Ind AS applicability** | **Roadmap dates **`1 APRIL 2016`** and **`1 APRIL 2017`**; **Ind AS 115 from **`1 APRIL 2018`**** | **SRC-22**, **SRC-23**, **SRC-24**, **SRC-25** — **Tier 2 and Tier 3, consistent** | **`CURRENT`. **The ₹250 crore and listing tests are corroborated across four sources but not against the primary Rules text — **`VERIFIED — SECONDARY`**** |

## III.3 What the date-control findings do to the architecture

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` and `[PROFESSIONAL QUESTION]`. **These consequences are recorded here and at Part XVIII. None of them amends the CEA.**

| # | **The consequence** | **Which architectural item it touches** | **Effect, using the four permitted labels** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Every tax proposition in the corpus, and every tax question in `ID-1`, was framed against the Income-tax Act, 1961. That Act is repealed for income of the current Tax Year. The nineteen tax heads at `ID-1` Part XIV and `TX-01 … TX-19` therefore need re-expressing against a statute whose section numbers have all changed, and whose new numbering is itself contested between secondary sources** | **`CQ-16 … CQ-18`; **`TX-01 … TX-19`; **`PVR-05`; **`SR-01`'s tax consequences; **`ID-5`**** | ### **`CLARIFICATION REQUIRED` — and, for every section number, `BLOCKED PENDING PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** |
| **2** | **The four pillars' labour consequences must now be worked out under four Codes in force since 21 November 2025, not under the Payment of Wages Act, the Minimum Wages Act, the Payment of Bonus Act, the Industrial Disputes Act, the EPF Act, the ESI Act, the Maternity Benefit Act or the Employees' Compensation Act as separate statutes. The Code on Wages now reaches **all employees** with no wage ceiling, which widens rather than narrows the architecture's exposure** | **`CQ-05 … CQ-09`; **`PLV-01 … PLV-16`; **`SR-02`; **`CD-01 … CD-21`; **`BLK-B-03`**** | ### **`ARCHITECTURAL AMENDMENT MAY BE REQUIRED` — the corpus cites repealed statutes by name** |
| **3** | **The data-protection obligations the architecture treats as open — `SR-05` fiduciary identity, `SR-06` retention with actual periods, `A-N14`, `PP-05`, `C-23`, `CQ-27` — sit under a statute whose core obligations are **not yet in force** as at the research date, and whose next phase falls due on or about 13 November 2026. This does not make the questions disappear: the definitions and the Board are in force, the obligations arrive on a known date, and other law continues to apply in the meantime** | **`CQ-27`; **`SR-05`, `SR-06`; **`A-N14`; **`PP-05`; **`C-23`; **`BLK-A-13`; **`L12`, `L13`**** | ### **`CLARIFICATION REQUIRED` — the question acquires a date, which it did not have** |
| **4** | **Criminal and procedural references in the corpus that were framed against the IPC, the CrPC and the Evidence Act are now references to the BNS, the BNSS and the BSA. The intervention boundary at `CQ-25`, the protection doctrine at Part XIII of this instrument, and the mandatory-reporting duties at `CQ-26` all engage provisions that have been renumbered** | **`CQ-25`, `CQ-26`; **`L1 … L15`; **`SR-31`; **`VG-1`, `VG-2`; **`A-09`**** | ### **`CLARIFICATION REQUIRED`** |
| **5** | **A research instrument prepared before October 2024, before November 2025, or before April 2026 would have been wrong on all three of the above. This is the strongest available argument for the corpus's own rule that provenance be established by search and not by recollection** | **Rule K-5; **`ID-1` §XX.5 step 6 — *"citations taken from memory: 0"*** | ### **`NO ARCHITECTURAL CHANGE` — the rule is vindicated** |

> ### **`THE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL FINDING OF THIS RESEARCH IS NOT ABOUT ANY SINGLE QUESTION. IT IS THAT THE STATUTORY GROUND UNDER EVERY TAX, LABOUR, DATA AND CRIMINAL QUESTION IN THE ARCHITECTURE CHANGED BETWEEN 2024 AND 2026, AND THAT ANY ANSWER GIVEN FROM RECOLLECTION WOULD HAVE BEEN AN ANSWER TO REPEALED LAW.`**
