# PART XI — TAX: THE NINETEEN HEADS, RESEARCHED

## XI.1 The rule this Part obeys

**[P]** Mandate §14 of this task. **For every tax item the mandate requires five things to be distinguished: **LAW**, **APPLICABILITY**, **FACT DEPENDENCY**, **ENTITY DEPENDENCY** and **PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION**. **And it forbids quoting a rate or threshold unless it has been verified against current authoritative material and the applicable facts. **Every figure below carries one of four tags.**

| Tag | **Meaning** | **What may be done with a figure so tagged** |
|---|---|---|
| ### **`VERIFIED — PRIMARY`** | **Located in a Tier 1 source — a government site, an official gazette, India Code, or an official judicial document** | **May be quoted with its source and date. **May still not be applied to Sewak Sathi, because application requires facts** |
| ### **`VERIFIED — SECONDARY`** | **Corroborated across two or more Tier 2 or Tier 3 sources, but not located in a Tier 1 source** | **May be quoted with its sources, its date, and an express statement that the primary text was not obtained** |
| ### **`SOURCE CONFLICT`** | **Two or more sources of comparable standing state different figures** | **Both are recorded at Part XVII. **Neither is preferred. **The figure is **not used**** |
| ### **`CURRENT-LAW PROFESSIONAL VERIFICATION REQUIRED`** | **The proposition may have changed, or the section number in the current statute is not established** | **May be described but not relied on** |

## XI.2 The findings

### `RF-35` — charitable purpose, the twenty-per-cent receipts test, and the exemption regime

| Field | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **`RESEARCH ID`** | **`RF-35`** |
| **`ARCHITECTURAL ID`** | **`TX-01 … TX-03`, `TX-06`, `TX-09`, `TX-10`; **`CQ-16`, `CQ-17`, `CQ-18`; **`GC-01`; **`SR-01`; **waterfall priorities 4, 5, 8; **the seven venture categories at **[S: Ch.17]**** |
| **`QUESTION`** | **What makes an organisation's purpose charitable for income-tax purposes, and what limits apply to commercial activity within it?** |
| **`LEGAL / TAX DOMAIN`** | **Tax** |
| **`RESEARCH FINDING`** | **The available sources, led by the Income Tax Department's own guidance, indicate the following. **Definition** — s.2(15) of the Income-tax Act provides an **inclusive** definition of *charitable purpose*, comprising **relief of the poor; **education; **yoga; **medical relief; **preservation of the environment, including watersheds, forests and wildlife; **preservation of monuments or places or objects of artistic or historic interest; **and **the advancement of any other object of general public utility. **The commercial limit** — the advancement of any other object of general public utility is **not** a charitable purpose where it involves carrying on any activity in the nature of **trade, commerce or business**, or any activity of **rendering any service in relation to any trade, commerce or business, for a fee, cess or other consideration**, unless two conditions are both satisfied: **that the activity is undertaken **in the course of the actual carrying out** of that advancement of general public utility; **and that **the aggregate receipts from such activity during the previous year do not exceed twenty per cent of the total receipts** of the trust or institution for that previous year. **The historical limits of ₹10 lakh and ₹25 lakh were replaced by the twenty-per-cent test, and s.13(8) was inserted with retrospective effect to deny exemption under s.11 or s.12 where the limit is exceeded. **Registration** — exemption is not automatic: **registration is required, and the current scheme is **s.12AB**, which replaced the earlier s.12AA regime. **Trusts and institutions granted perpetual registration before **1 April 2021** were required to apply for re-registration under s.12AB. **Registration under s.12AB is valid for **five years**, and for **ten years** where the total income before exemption does not exceed **₹5 crore** in each of the two previous years preceding the year of application. **Renewal must be applied for **at least six months before** the completion of the five years. **A provisional registration must be converted into a normal registration by an application in **Form 10AB** at least six months before the expiry of the provisional period, or **within six months of commencement of activities, whichever is earlier**. **Where registration has become inoperative, the application to make it operative again must be made **at least six months before the assessment year** from which it is sought. **Application of income** — under s.11(2), where a trust is unable to apply **eighty-five per cent** of its income in a particular year, it may accumulate the shortfall for application to religious or charitable purposes **within the next five years**, provided the Assessing Officer is informed of the purpose and the period of accumulation. **Exemption is denied** for anonymous donations taxable under s.115BBC, and **where the s.2(15) proviso is violated**. **Audit** — accounts must be audited where income exceeds the basic exemption limit, with the report in **Form 10B** for s.11 and **Form 10BB** for s.10(23C). **Section 10(23C)** is a separate route available only to **specified** entities such as universities, educational institutions and hospitals. **The cost-recovery distinction** — a decision of the Gujarat High Court, *Commissioner of Income Tax (Exemptions) v. Ahmedabad Urban Development Authority*, reported at 2023 (9) TMI 986, records that where a statutory body, while actually furthering a general-public-utility object, carries on activities entailing some trade, commerce or business generating profit, it can still be characterised as a general-public-utility charity provided the quantum of such receipts is within the twenty-per-cent limit; **that amounts which merely cover expenditure, including administrative and other costs plus a small proportion for provision, are **not** consideration towards trade, commerce or business or a service in relation thereto; **and that amounts **significantly higher than recovery of costs** must be treated as receipts from trade, commerce or business, for which the twenty-per-cent limit applies and for which **separate books of account** must be maintained. **Continuity into the current statute** — a 2026 source records that under the Income-tax Act, 2025 the same three conditions persist: **the activity must be undertaken while actually carrying out the public-utility objective; **commercial receipts must not exceed twenty per cent of total receipts for the relevant **tax year**; **and **separate books of account** must be maintained for those activities** |
| **`PRIMARY SOURCE`** | ### **`SRC-32 — Income Tax Department, Government of India: *"Taxability of income of charitable or religious trusts"*, giving the s.2(15) definition, the s.12AB registration scheme with its five-year and ten-year validity, the renewal and Form 10AB timings, the s.11(2) eighty-five-per-cent rule and five-year accumulation, the s.115BBC and proviso denials, and the Form 10B and 10BB audit reports. **`TIER 1`**. **`SRC-34` — reports the Gujarat High Court decision with its cost-recovery reasoning. **`TIER 2`**. **`SRC-33`**, **`SRC-35`**, **`SRC-36`** — corroborating** |
| **`SOURCE DATE / EFFECTIVE DATE`** | **The Income Tax Department page carries no publication date and was accessed on the research date; **its content is expressed in terms of the 1961 Act and of s.12AB, and refers to applications made on or before **30 September 2023**, which places its drafting after that date. **The Gujarat High Court decision is reported for **2023**. **The 2026 continuity statement is **`VERIFIED — SECONDARY`**. **`CURRENT`, with **`DTC-01`** applying to every section number: **the 2025 Act renumbers the whole statute, and the new numbering for this subject matter is in conflict — **`SCF-02`**** |
| **`RELEVANT CONDITION`** | ### **`THE TWENTY-PER-CENT TEST IS THE ARCHITECTURALLY DECISIVE CONDITION, AND IT HAS THREE LIMBS: the activity must be in the nature of trade, commerce or business **or** a service rendered in relation to one **for a fee, cess or other consideration**; **it must be undertaken in the course of actually carrying out the public-utility object; **and its aggregate receipts must not exceed twenty per cent of total receipts for the year. **The cost-recovery distinction is a fourth, judicially developed limb: **receipts that merely recover cost are not trade receipts, and receipts significantly above cost are. **Registration under s.12AB is a precondition of exemption, and it is time-limited** |
| **`ENTITY DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES` — the exemption regime is addressed to a *trust or institution*, and the registration route differs by form** |
| **`FACT DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES` — total receipts, the split between cost-recovery and above-cost receipts, and whether any activity is undertaken in the course of the public-utility object. **None of these facts exists** |
| **`INTERPRETATION RISK`** | ### **`HIGH` — *"significantly higher than recovery of costs"* is not quantified in the material located, and the architecture's **organisation service fee at waterfall priority 5** is precisely a charge whose relationship to cost is undefined because no cost figure exists** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE ACTUALLY ESTABLISHES`** | **That charitable status for tax purposes is **inclusive but bounded**; **that the boundary is a **quantitative** test of twenty per cent of total receipts, not a qualitative test of intention; **that **cost-recovery charges sit outside the test** while **above-cost charges sit inside it**; **that exemption requires **registration**, which is **time-limited** and must be **renewed**; **that **eighty-five per cent** of income must be applied, with a five-year accumulation route; **and that **separate books of account** are required for the commercial activity** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE DOES NOT ESTABLISH`** | **That this architecture's activities are charitable. **That the four pillars fall within the s.2(15) heads — **the protection pillar and the AI-business pillar are not obviously within *relief of the poor*, *education*, *yoga*, *medical relief*, *preservation of the environment* or *preservation of monuments*, and would have to fall within *advancement of any other object of general public utility*, which is exactly the head that carries the twenty-per-cent limit**. **That exemption would be available. **What *"significantly higher"* means. **And the current section numbers** |
| **`SEWAK SATHI CONSEQUENCE`** | ### **`CLARIFICATION REQUIRED`, AND IT IS THE SINGLE MOST USEFUL TAX FINDING IN THIS RESEARCH. **`GC-01` asks whether the eight-priority waterfall can coexist with *"restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside"*. **This finding does not resolve **`GC-01`**, but it **re-frames** it in a way the architecture has never had: **the restriction that tax law actually imposes on a general-public-utility organisation is not a prohibition on distributions — it is a **quantitative ceiling on commercial receipts** and a **cost-recovery discipline**. **That means the live question for waterfall priority 5, the organisation service fee, is not *"may it exist?"* but *"does it merely recover cost, or is it significantly above cost, and does the aggregate exceed twenty per cent of total receipts?"* — and the answer to that is **unanswerable while `V-01 … V-22` are empty**, because the test is arithmetic and no arithmetic exists. **Priority 4 and priority 8, by contrast, are **compensation** and **upside**, and neither is obviously a *receipt from trade, commerce or business*, so the twenty-per-cent test does not obviously reach them — but **`RF-36`** and the s.13 denial provisions may, and that is a professional question. **This is recorded at **`ACN-04`** as an architectural amendment that may be required, and **not applied**** |
| **`PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | **`YES`** |
| **`CLASSIFICATION`** | ### **`SUPPORTED BY PRIMARY SOURCE` — with the cost-recovery limb `SUPPORTED BY SECONDARY SOURCE ONLY` and the 2025 Act continuity limb `VERIFIED — SECONDARY`** |

### `RF-36` — the donor's deduction, and why it may be worth nothing

| Field | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **`RESEARCH ID`** | **`RF-36`** |
| **`ARCHITECTURAL ID`** | **`TX-06`, `TX-11`; **`CQ-16`, `CQ-17`; **`DIS-14`; **funding of pillar 1; **CEA §XII.2 the funding map; **`G-16` grant capture** |
| **`QUESTION`** | **What deduction does a donor obtain for a donation, and what must the recipient hold for the donor to obtain it?** |
| **`LEGAL / TAX DOMAIN`** | **Tax** |
| **`RESEARCH FINDING`** | **The available sources indicate that s.80G of the Income-tax Act provides a deduction from gross total income for donations to eligible funds, charitable institutions and approved organisations, in **four categories**: **one hundred per cent deduction without qualifying limit; **fifty per cent deduction without qualifying limit; **one hundred per cent deduction subject to a qualifying limit of **ten per cent of adjusted gross total income**; **and fifty per cent deduction subject to that same ten-per-cent limit. **Donations to specified national funds fall in the unlimited categories — the National Defence Fund, the Prime Minister's National Relief Fund, the Prime Minister's CARES Fund, the National Children's Fund and the National Foundation for Communal Harmony are given as examples of one hundred per cent without limit, and the Prime Minister's Drought Relief Fund as an example of fifty per cent without limit. **Donations to **public charitable trusts and approved non-governmental organisations** typically fall in the **fifty per cent** categories, with or without the qualifying limit depending on the organisation's status. **Where the qualifying limit applies, the deductible amount is computed on the lesser of the donation and ten per cent of adjusted gross total income, and the excess is ignored and **cannot be carried forward**. **The total deductions under Chapter VI-A cannot exceed gross total income, so the deduction may reduce taxable income to nil but **cannot create or increase a loss**. **Cash donations above **₹2,000** are **not eligible**; **eligible modes include bank transfer, cheque, demand draft, debit or credit card and authorised digital payment. **The recipient must be **approved and registered**, and the donor requires a donation receipt and **Form 10BE** issued by the approved institution. **Two sources record a further condition of decisive practical importance: **the Chapter VI-A deductions, including s.80G, are **not available to a taxpayer who is taxed under the default new regime**, which is the default for the current tax year** |
| **`PRIMARY SOURCE`** | **`SRC-94`**, **`SRC-95`**, **`SRC-96`**, **`SRC-97`** — **Tier 3**, mutually consistent on the four categories, the ₹2,000 cash limit, Form 10BE and the ten-per-cent qualifying limit. **`SRC-98`** — **Tier 3**, for the unavailability of Chapter VI-A deductions under the default regime. **No Tier 1 source was located for s.80G in this research** |
| **`SOURCE DATE / EFFECTIVE DATE`** | **The sources describe the position for FY 2024-25 and FY 2025-26 and note that s.80G as such applies to donations relating to FY 2025-26 and earlier, with the 2025 Act renumbering the deduction from 1 April 2026. **`CURRENT APPLICABILITY NOT ESTABLISHED` as to the section number in the current statute — **two sources give different new numbers, recorded as **`SCF-02`**** |
| **`RELEVANT CONDITION`** | ### **`FOUR: the recipient must be **approved**; **the donation must be in an **eligible mode**, cash above ₹2,000 being ineligible; **the **ten-per-cent qualifying limit** applies to two of the four categories; **and — on the available secondary source — **the donor must not be taxed under the default regime**, under which Chapter VI-A deductions are unavailable** |
| **`ENTITY DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES` — approval is granted to an entity, and no entity exists** |
| **`FACT DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES` — whether any donation is contemplated, from whom, and in what mode. **`DIS-14` is blank** |
| **`INTERPRETATION RISK`** | **`MEDIUM` — the four categories and the cash limit are consistent across four sources; **the regime-availability condition rests on one** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE ACTUALLY ESTABLISHES`** | **That a donor's deduction is **not a property of the donation** but of the **recipient's approval status**, the **mode**, the **donor's own income**, and the **donor's chosen tax regime**; **that an unapproved recipient confers **no deduction at all**; **and that cash above a stated small limit is outside the provision entirely** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE DOES NOT ESTABLISH`** | **That approval would be granted. **That donations would follow from approval. **The current section number. **And it does not establish that the **recipient** is taxed on the donation — which is a separate question governed by **`RF-35`** |
| **`SEWAK SATHI CONSEQUENCE`** | ### **`CLARIFICATION REQUIRED`, AND IT QUALIFIES AN ASSUMPTION THE ARCHITECTURE HAS NOT EXAMINED. **The funding map at CEA §XII.2 and the corpus's treatment of donations proceed on the basis that donation income is available to pillar 1. **This research does not disturb that, but it records two conditions that materially affect it: **approval is a **separate** registration from the exemption registration, so an entity can be exempt and still confer no donor benefit; **and, on the available secondary source, a donor taxed under the **default** regime obtains **no** deduction whatever the recipient's status. **If that second condition is correct, then the value of donor-deduction approval to this architecture is **materially lower than an unqualified reading of s.80G would suggest** — and the proposition is recorded as requiring professional validation, not as a conclusion** |
| **`PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | **`YES`** |
| **`CLASSIFICATION`** | ### **`SUPPORTED BY SECONDARY SOURCE ONLY`** |

### `RF-37` — tax deduction at source on contractor and professional payments

| Field | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **`RESEARCH ID`** | **`RF-37`** |
| **`ARCHITECTURAL ID`** | **`TX-04`, `TX-05`, `TX-12`, `TX-13`; **`CQ-18`, `CQ-33`; **`CD-17`; **`PLV-15`; **`AH-14`; **waterfall priorities 1, 2, 3** |
| **`QUESTION`** | **What deduction at source applies to payments for work and for professional services?** |
| **`LEGAL / TAX DOMAIN`** | **Tax** |
| **`RESEARCH FINDING`** | **The available sources indicate, for payments to residents: **contractor payments** — under s.194C of the Income-tax Act, 1961, tax is deducted on payments to contractors and sub-contractors for work, at **one per cent** where the payee is a resident individual or a Hindu undivided family and **two per cent** where the payee is any other resident, including a company, firm, limited liability partnership, association of persons, co-operative or trust; **the threshold is **₹30,000 for a single payment or ₹1,00,000 in aggregate during the financial year**; **where the payee does not furnish a valid Permanent Account Number the rate is **twenty per cent** under the higher-rate rule formerly at s.206AA; **transport contractors are exempt where they own **ten or fewer** goods carriages and furnish their Permanent Account Number; **and the provision reaches sub-contractors, so the deduction flows down the chain, and is recorded as now expressly including **manpower supply** — security, housekeeping and staffing contracts being deducted at the contractor rate rather than as professional fees. **Professional and technical fees** — under s.194J, tax is deducted at **ten per cent** for professional services, including fees paid to legal, medical, accounting, architectural, engineering and consultancy professionals, and at **two per cent** for specified technical services, royalties and non-compete fees; **the threshold is stated **differently across sources**: **₹30,000 in three sources and **₹50,000 per year with effect from 1 April 2025** in two others — recorded as **`SCF-04`** and not resolved. **Other sections located, for completeness: **s.194I** on rent at ten per cent for land and building and two per cent for machinery and equipment, with a threshold of ₹2.4 lakh; **s.194A** on interest at ten per cent, or twenty per cent without a Permanent Account Number, with thresholds of ₹40,000 and ₹50,000 for senior citizens; **s.194H** on commission and brokerage at five per cent with a threshold of ₹15,000; **and s.194-IB** on rent paid by an individual or Hindu undivided family not liable to audit, at two per cent where monthly rent exceeds ₹50,000. **Deduction is on the base value where GST is itemised separately. **Under the Income-tax Act, 2025** — sources record that resident tax-deduction provisions are **consolidated into s.393**, that **s.194C becomes a contractor-payments row of s.393 with its own payment code**, that **salary deduction moves from s.192 to s.392(1)**, that **tax collection at source sits in s.394**, that **rates and thresholds carry over substantially unchanged**, and that **Form 140 replaces Form 26Q**, with a separate source recording that **Form 138 replaces Form 24Q**** |
| **`PRIMARY SOURCE`** | **`SRC-90`**, **`SRC-92`** — **Tier 3**, consistent on the s.194C rates, thresholds, no-PAN rate, transporter exemption and the mapping to s.393. **`SRC-89`**, **`SRC-93`**, **`SRC-91`** — **Tier 3**, on s.194J and the other sections. **`SRC-06`**, **`SRC-08`** — on the 2025 Act consolidation. **No Tier 1 source was located for any deduction provision in this research** |
| **`SOURCE DATE / EFFECTIVE DATE`** | **The 1961 Act provisions applied until 31 March 2026; **the 2025 Act applies from **1 April 2026**. **The s.194J threshold change is dated **1 April 2025** by the sources that record it. **`CURRENT APPLICABILITY NOT ESTABLISHED` for the s.194J threshold and for every 2025 Act section number** |
| **`RELEVANT CONDITION`** | **Resident payee; **payment for **work** as against **professional or technical services**, the distinction between which determines both the section and the rate; **the thresholds, which are per-payment and aggregate; **a valid Permanent Account Number; **and, for transport, ownership of ten or fewer goods carriages** |
| **`ENTITY DEPENDENCY`** | **`PARTLY` — the obligation attaches to a **payer** of the specified description, and whether this architecture's eventual entity is such a payer depends on its form and on the nature of the payment** |
| **`FACT DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES` — the nature of every payment, the payee's status, and whether any payment is made at all. **No payment can be made: **`C-15`**** |
| **`INTERPRETATION RISK`** | ### **`HIGH` — the s.194J threshold is in conflict, and the distinction between *work* and *professional services* determines whether the rate is one to two per cent or ten per cent, which for the architecture's trainers, specialists and coordinators is **the** classification question in tax form** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE ACTUALLY ESTABLISHES`** | **That every payment for work or services above a stated threshold carries a withholding obligation at a stated rate; **that the rate is **higher where the payee has no Permanent Account Number**; **that sub-contracting does not avoid the obligation; **and that manpower supply is now expressly within the contractor provision** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE DOES NOT ESTABLISH`** | **The s.194J threshold. **The current section numbers. **Which of the architecture's roles would be *work* and which *professional services* — which is the same question as **`SR-02`**, in tax form. **And it does not establish that any withholding would arise, because no payment can be made** |
| **`SEWAK SATHI CONSEQUENCE`** | ### **`CLARIFICATION REQUIRED`, AND IT ADDS A COST THE WATERFALL DOES NOT CARRY. **Waterfall priority 2 is *"statutory dues where applicable"*, and the corpus treats it as an obligation of the **worker's** side. **This research establishes a **second** statutory deduction that sits on the **payer's** side and is triggered by the very act of paying at priority 1 — together with a **twenty-per-cent** rate where the payee has no Permanent Account Number, which for informal and first-time workers is a realistic case. **`ID-1` **`CD-17`** asked about reimbursement and **`PLV-15`** about withholding; **both now have researched content, and neither has an answer. **The architectural consequence is recorded at **`ACN-07`**: **priority 2 cannot be costed without a withholding assumption, and no such assumption exists** |
| **`PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | **`YES`** |
| **`CLASSIFICATION`** | ### **`PARTIALLY SUPPORTED` — s.194C by consistent secondary sources; **s.194J `SOURCE CONFLICT`; **every 2025 Act section number `CURRENT-LAW PROFESSIONAL VERIFICATION REQUIRED`** |

### `RF-38` — the GST exemption for charitable activities, and its two cumulative conditions

| Field | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **`RESEARCH ID`** | **`RF-38`** |
| **`ARCHITECTURAL ID`** | **`TX-07`, `TX-08`, `TX-09`; **`REV-1 … REV-4`; **`CQ-17`, `CQ-20`; **`GC-01`; **the seven venture categories; **pillar 1 and pillar 4** |
| **`QUESTION`** | **Is a charitable organisation's activity exempt from GST, and on what conditions?** |
| **`LEGAL / TAX DOMAIN`** | **GST** |
| **`RESEARCH FINDING`** | **The available sources indicate that **Notification No. 12/2017-Central Tax (Rate), dated 28 June 2017**, exempts, at **entry 1**, *"services by an entity registered under section 12AA"* — reported by one source as extended to *"sections 12AA / 12AB"* — of the Income-tax Act, 1961, **by way of charitable activities**, from the whole of the GST. **Two conditions are **cumulative**: **the entity must hold the income-tax registration; **and the activity must be a **charitable activity** as defined in the notification, which means activities relating to **public health, by way of care or counselling of persons specified in the notification — including the terminally ill, the physically disabled, persons affected with HIV or AIDS and alcohol-dependent persons — and public awareness of preventive health, family planning or prevention of HIV infection; **the **advancement of religion, spirituality or yoga**, subject to the notification's own conditions on fees and on residential programmes; **the **preservation of the environment, including watershed, forests and wildlife**; **and **advancement of educational programmes or skill development** relating to specified categories, including abandoned, orphaned or homeless children, physically or mentally abused persons, prisoners, and persons over the age of sixty-five residing in a rural area. **Related exemptions located include **entry 13**, for services by a person by way of specified activities for the **general public**, the term being defined in clause (zc) as *"the body of people at large sufficiently defined by some common quality of public or impersonal nature"*; **entry 50**, excluding public libraries that lend books and knowledge-enhancing content; **entry 66**, for services by and to educational institutions; **entry 80**, for training or coaching in recreational activities relating to **arts or culture** or to **sports** by charitable entities registered under s.12AA; **and, in the integrated-tax notification, **entry 10 of Notification No. 9/2017-Integrated Tax (Rate)**, under which services received by a s.12AA-registered charitable trust from a provider located in a non-taxable territory, for charitable purposes, are not chargeable under the reverse charge mechanism. **What is **not** exempt, on the sources located: **the **sale of goods** by a charitable trust, which is taxable; **services that are not within the definition of charitable activities; **renting of premises above the notification's stated limits — with a limited exemption for rooms in a religious place charged below a stated daily amount, and for a hall or open area charged below a stated daily amount; **sponsorship, which is taxable under the reverse charge mechanism; **and advertising rights during events** |
| **`PRIMARY SOURCE`** | **`SRC-82`** — reproduces the Central Board of Excise and Customs guide, which is the predecessor authority to CBIC. **`SRC-80`, `SRC-81`, `SRC-83`** — **Tier 3**, consistent on the notification number, date, entry 1, the two cumulative conditions and the definition. **The notification itself was **not obtained from a CBIC or GST Council source in this research**** |
| **`SOURCE DATE / EFFECTIVE DATE`** | **Notification No. 12/2017-Central Tax (Rate), dated **28 June 2017**; **Notification No. 9/2017-Integrated Tax (Rate), same date. **`CURRENT APPLICABILITY NOT ESTABLISHED` — neither notification was verified against a CBIC source, both have been amended repeatedly since 2017, and one source states the entry as covering *"sections 12AA / 12AB"* while two state *"section 12AA"* alone, which suggests an amendment whose date was not located** |
| **`RELEVANT CONDITION`** | ### **`TWO CUMULATIVE CONDITIONS, AND BOTH ARE UNMET HERE: (1) registration under the income-tax provision — which requires an entity, and which **`RF-35`** shows is now s.12AB and is **time-limited**; **and (2) the activity must fall within the notification's own **closed definition** of charitable activities, which is narrower than s.2(15) of the Income-tax Act and does not obviously include a first-response or protection activity, an AI-business activity, or a community-network activity** |
| **`ENTITY DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES` — condition (1) requires an entity holding a registration** |
| **`FACT DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES` — what is actually supplied, to whom, and for what consideration. **The seven venture categories at **[S: Ch.17]** mix goods and services, and **`RF-14`** shows that the two are treated differently** |
| **`INTERPRETATION RISK`** | ### **`HIGH — the definition is closed and its heads are specific. Whether *public health*, *advancement of education* or *the general public* reaches a mohalla-level service, a protection activity or an AI service is **not addressed by any source located**, and the temptation to read the exemption broadly is exactly what mandate §21 forbids** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE ACTUALLY ESTABLISHES`** | **That a **complete** exemption exists, but only for **services**, only for an entity holding a specified income-tax registration, and only for activities within a **closed statutory definition**; **that the **sale of goods is outside it**; **that renting, sponsorship and advertising are outside it except within narrow limits; **and that a related reverse-charge relief exists for imported services received for charitable purposes** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE DOES NOT ESTABLISH`** | ### **`That any activity of this architecture is exempt. **That the protection pillar, the AI-business pillar or the community-network pillar falls within the definition — **none of the four pillars maps cleanly onto the notification's heads, and this instrument does not attempt a mapping**. **That the exemption is available without registration — **it is not, and no registration exists because no entity exists. **And it does not establish that *"GST does not apply"*, a formulation prohibited by mandate §21 and not used anywhere in this instrument`** |
| **`SEWAK SATHI CONSEQUENCE`** | ### **`CLARIFICATION REQUIRED`, AND IT ADDS A REGISTRATION SEQUENCE THE ARCHITECTURE DID NOT HAVE. **`ID-1` **`TX-07`** asked about the GST position and **`TX-08`** about exemptions. **This research establishes that the exemption is **conditional on an income-tax registration that is itself conditional on an entity**, so the sequence is: **entity → income-tax registration → GST exemption**, and not the reverse. **It also establishes a **`GC-01`** consequence the conflict analysis did not have: **if a commercial venture's services fall outside the notification's closed definition, they are **taxable** supplies, and **`RF-39`** shows that inter-state supply of goods requires registration **irrespective of turnover** — so a national organisation selling goods across states would be inside the GST system from its first rupee. **That is recorded at **`ACN-05`** and not applied** |
| **`PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | **`YES`** |
| **`CLASSIFICATION`** | ### **`PARTIALLY SUPPORTED` — the notification and its two conditions by four consistent sources; **the current text and the 12AA-versus-12AB amendment `NOT ESTABLISHED`** |

### `RF-39` — GST registration: the thresholds, and the cases where no threshold applies

| Field | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **`RESEARCH ID`** | **`RF-39`** |
| **`ARCHITECTURAL ID`** | **`TX-07`; **`REV-2`, `REV-4`; **`CQ-17`, `CQ-20`; **`SR-01`; **`BLK-B-02`; **the seven venture categories** |
| **`QUESTION`** | **When must a person register for GST, and when does turnover not matter?** |
| **`LEGAL / TAX DOMAIN`** | **GST** |
| **`RESEARCH FINDING`** | **The available sources indicate that **s.22(1) of the CGST Act, 2017** requires every supplier to be registered in the state or union territory, other than a special category state, **from where he makes a taxable supply** of goods or services or both, where his **aggregate turnover in a financial year exceeds ₹40 lakh for goods or ₹20 lakh for services**. **For special category states the thresholds are lower — ₹20 lakh for goods and ₹10 lakh for services in the states listed, and ₹10 lakh for both in Manipur, Mizoram, Nagaland and Tripura. **Registration is required **only in the state or union territory from which the taxable supply is made**, and not in every state where customers are located, provided there is no fixed place of business there. ***Aggregate turnover* is the total value of all supplies made under the **same Permanent Account Number** across India, and **includes taxable supplies, exempt supplies, exports and inter-state supplies**, but excludes the GST taxes themselves and inward supplies taxable under reverse charge; **the ₹40 lakh goods threshold is computed excluding interest on deposits, loans and advances. **The ₹40 lakh threshold is **not available** where registration is compulsory, where the person deals in specified goods such as ice cream, pan masala and tobacco, where the person is in a special category state, where voluntary registration has been taken, where handicraft goods are supplied inter-state, or for job workers — in each of which cases the ₹20 lakh threshold applies. **Section 24** then provides for **compulsory registration irrespective of turnover**, beginning with a non-obstante clause that overrides s.22(1), and covering persons making **inter-state taxable supplies**; **casual taxable persons; **non-resident taxable persons; **persons required to pay tax under **reverse charge**; **e-commerce operators and persons supplying through them; **persons required to deduct or collect tax at source; **suppliers of online information and database access or retrieval services; **suppliers of online money gaming from outside India; **and, on one source, agents and such other classes as are notified. **An exception is recorded for **inter-state supplies of taxable services**: **Notification No. 10/2017-Integrated Tax dated 13 October 2017** exempts persons making inter-state supplies of **taxable services** with an aggregate turnover not exceeding ₹20 lakh — or ₹10 lakh in the specified states — from the requirement to register. **The penalty for failure to register where required is recorded as **ten per cent of the tax due or ₹10,000, whichever is higher**. **Registration is recorded as being granted within **three working days** of a complete application** |
| **`PRIMARY SOURCE`** | **`SRC-88`** — **Tier 3**, the fullest account, citing s.22, the special-category-state table, the composition of aggregate turnover, the exclusions from the ₹40 lakh threshold, s.24 and Notification No. 10/2017-Integrated Tax by number and date. **`SRC-84`, `SRC-85`, `SRC-86`, `SRC-87`** — **Tier 3**, consistent. **No CBIC or GST Council source was obtained in this research** |
| **`SOURCE DATE / EFFECTIVE DATE`** | **CGST Act, 2017, in force from 1 July 2017; **Notification No. 10/2017-Integrated Tax dated **13 October 2017**. **`CURRENT`, but **not verified against a CBIC source**, and the GST rate structure and thresholds have been amended repeatedly since 2017** |
| **`RELEVANT CONDITION`** | ### **`THREE. **(1) The supply must be **taxable** — **`RF-38`** determines whether it is. **(2) **Aggregate turnover is computed **per Permanent Account Number across all of India**, and **includes exempt supplies**, so an organisation whose activities are wholly exempt still counts them toward the threshold. **(3) **Section 24 displaces the threshold entirely for the listed categories, of which **inter-state supply of goods** is the one most likely to arise for a national organisation** |
| **`ENTITY DEPENDENCY`** | **`PARTLY` — the obligation attaches to a *person* making a taxable supply, and a Permanent Account Number is required, which requires an entity** |
| **`FACT DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES` — turnover, the state of supply, and whether goods or services or both are supplied** |
| **`INTERPRETATION RISK`** | **`MEDIUM` — the structure is clear and consistently reported; **the state lists and the s.24 categories were not verified against the Act** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE ACTUALLY ESTABLISHES`** | **That GST registration is **threshold-based in principle and threshold-free in eight or more specified cases**; **that the threshold is computed **nationally and per Permanent Account Number** and **includes exempt supplies**; **that registration follows the **place of supply**, not the place of the customer; **and that **inter-state supply of goods** requires registration from the first rupee** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE DOES NOT ESTABLISH`** | **That registration is required here. **No supply, no turnover and no Permanent Account Number exists. **It does not establish that an exempt-supply organisation is outside the system — **`RF-38`** and the composition of aggregate turnover point the other way** |
| **`SEWAK SATHI CONSEQUENCE`** | ### **`CLARIFICATION REQUIRED`, AND IT IS A CONSTRAINT ON SCALE THAT THE ARCHITECTURE HAS NOT RECORDED. **The corpus contemplates a national architecture with state and city layers — **`C-11`, `C-12`** prohibit both at present — and a venture model that supplies goods. **This research establishes that **any inter-state supply of goods requires GST registration irrespective of turnover**, and that aggregate turnover is counted **per Permanent Account Number across all states**, including exempt supplies. **A national operation therefore cannot stay outside the GST system by staying small, and cannot stay outside it by being exempt. **This is recorded at **`ACN-06`** and bears directly on **`CQ-34`** — state heterogeneity — and on **`FC-E6`**** |
| **`PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | **`YES`** |
| **`CLASSIFICATION`** | ### **`SUPPORTED BY SECONDARY SOURCE ONLY`** |

### `RF-40` — data protection: what is in force now, and what is not yet

| Field | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **`RESEARCH ID`** | **`RF-40`** |
| **`ARCHITECTURAL ID`** | **`SR-05`, `SR-06`; **`A-N14`; **`PP-05`; **`C-23`; **`CQ-27`; **`BLK-A-13`; **`L12`, `L13`; **`TX-16`; **`AH-13`** |
| **`QUESTION`** | **What data-protection obligations apply now, and when do the rest begin?** |
| **`LEGAL / TAX DOMAIN`** | **Data and privacy** |
| **`RESEARCH FINDING`** | **The available sources indicate that the Digital Personal Data Protection Act, 2023 was commenced in phases by notification **`G.S.R. 843(E)` dated 13 November 2025**, published in the Official Gazette on **14 November 2025**, and that the Digital Personal Data Protection Rules, 2025 were notified by **`G.S.R. 844(E)`, `845(E)` and `846(E)`** of the same date, published the following day. **In force now** — s.1(2) and s.2, the definitions; **ss.18 to 26**, which establish the **Data Protection Board of India** and its procedures; **s.35; **ss.38 to 43; **and s.44(1) and (3). **In the Rules, Rules 1, 2 and 17 to 21 came into force on publication. **One year from publication — on or about **13 November 2026** — s.6(9) and s.27(1)(d), and Rule 4, which govern the **registration and obligations of a Consent Manager**; **the sources record that a Consent Manager must be an **Indian company with a minimum net worth of ₹2 crore**, must provide an accessible, transparent and interoperable platform for a Data Principal to give, manage, review and withdraw consent, and is solely responsible to the Data Principal. **Eighteen months from publication — on or about **13 May 2027** — the **core conduct and enforcement provisions**: **ss.3 to 5; **s.6(1) to (8) and (10); **ss.7 to 17; **s.27 except 27(1)(d); **ss.28 to 34; **ss.36 and 37; **and s.44(2). **In the Rules, Rules 3, 5 to 16, 22 and 23. **The obligations that commence at that date are recorded as including **notice and consent** — a privacy notice to each Data Principal specifying the personal data collected, the purpose of processing, and how the Data Principal may withdraw consent with ease comparable to giving it, exercise statutory rights and complain to the Board; **legitimate uses; **children's data; **the obligations of a **Significant Data Fiduciary**, including annual data-protection impact assessments and the appointment of a Data Protection Officer; **the handling of Data Principal rights; **appeals; **penalties; **and the central blocking power. **Security safeguards are recorded as requiring reasonable measures including **encryption, obfuscation, masking, access control and detection of unauthorised access**; **contractual obligations are recorded as requiring **valid contracts with appropriate data-protection provisions** for personal data transferred to a Data Processor for further processing; **retention is recorded as requiring **erasure on completion of the purpose** for which the data was collected unless retention is mandated by law; **and breach reporting is recorded as carrying a **72-hour** timeline. **Grievance redressal is recorded as requiring a response within a period **not exceeding 90 days** from receipt. **The consultation preceding the Rules received **6,915 inputs**** |
| **`PRIMARY SOURCE`** | **`SRC-13`** — **PwC India regulatory insights, 16 November 2025**, **`TIER 2`**, citing the gazette numbers. **`SRC-14`, `SRC-15`** — **Tier 2**, giving the phased commencement architecture section by section and the three operative dates. **`SRC-17`** — **Taxmann analysis**, **`TIER 2`**, confirming that all four notifications are dated 13 November 2025 and published on 14 November 2025. **`SRC-16`** — **Tier 3**, giving the section-by-section in-force status. **The Act, the Rules and the gazette notifications themselves were **not obtained from a MeitY or India Code source in this research**** |
| **`SOURCE DATE / EFFECTIVE DATE`** | **Act No. 22 of 2023, assent **11 August 2023**. **Draft Rules **3 January 2025**. **Final Rules and commencement notifications **13 November 2025**, published **14 November 2025**. **Phase 2 on or about **13 November 2026**. **Phase 3 on or about **13 May 2027**. **`CURRENT — AND TWO OF THE THREE PHASES ARE FUTURE-DATED RELATIVE TO THE RESEARCH DATE OF 8 OCTOBER 2026`** |
| **`RELEVANT CONDITION`** | ### **`THE OBLIGATIONS ATTACH TO A **DATA FIDUCIARY**, A TERM DEFINED IN s.2, WHICH IS IN FORCE. **The core duties do not attach until Phase 3. **Whether this architecture is or would be a Data Fiduciary is **not established**, because **`SR-05`** records that **fiduciary identity remains open** and **`PP-05`** forbids inferring it from the collection of data — and collection is itself prohibited by **`C-23`**** |
| **`ENTITY DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES` for fiduciary status, which requires a person on whom duties can fall. **`NO` for the design of a record, which is why **`CQ-27`** carries a qualified `NO` in `ID-1`** |
| **`FACT DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES` — whether any personal data is held anywhere. **`DIS-22` is blank** |
| **`INTERPRETATION RISK`** | **`MEDIUM` — the commencement architecture is consistently reported by four sources citing the same gazette numbers; **the content of the obligations is reported by secondary sources and the Rules were not obtained** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE ACTUALLY ESTABLISHES`** | **That a **regulator exists now**; **that the **definitions are in force now**; **that the **substantive duties are not yet in force**, and commence on a **known future date**; **that a **Consent Manager** registration layer commences on an **earlier known future date**, approximately five weeks after this research date; **and that the duties, when they commence, are specific as to notice content, consent withdrawal, security safeguards, processor contracts, erasure, breach reporting within 72 hours and grievance response within 90 days** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE DOES NOT ESTABLISH`** | **That this architecture is a Data Fiduciary. **That any obligation is currently breached — **no data is held and none may be collected, **`C-23`**. **The penalty amounts, which were **not located in this research**. **And it does not establish that the absence of a current obligation is a reason to defer: **the commencement date is fixed, and the obligations are known** |
| **`SEWAK SATHI CONSEQUENCE`** | ### **`CLARIFICATION REQUIRED`, AND IT GIVES `SR-06` SOMETHING IT HAS NEVER HAD: **A DATE`. **`ID-1` recorded that **`CQ-27`**'s record design needs no entity while fiduciary status does, and that **`SR-06`** requires *"actual periods"* which no source supplied. **This research supplies the shape of the obligation and the date from which it bites, and it establishes that the design work at **`CQ-27`** can be done **now**, before the duties commence, which is the cheapest possible time to do it. **It also establishes that **`SR-05`** — naming a privacy officer and identifying a fiduciary — has a statutory analogue in the Significant Data Fiduciary's Data Protection Officer, though whether this architecture would ever be a Significant Data Fiduciary is **not established**. **Recorded at **`ACN-08`** and not applied** |
| **`PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | **`YES` — the Rules text, the penalty provisions, and whether any Data Fiduciary duty attaches before Phase 3** |
| **`CLASSIFICATION`** | ### **`PARTIALLY SUPPORTED` — the commencement architecture by four corroborating Tier 2 sources citing the gazette numbers; **the content of the obligations `SUPPORTED BY SECONDARY SOURCE ONLY`** |

### `RF-41` — the current income-tax statute's treatment of non-profit organisations, and a conflict in its numbering

| Field | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **`RESEARCH ID`** | **`RF-41`** |
| **`ARCHITECTURAL ID`** | **`TX-01 … TX-03`, `TX-06`, `TX-09`, `TX-10`; **`CQ-16`, `CQ-17`; **`SR-01`; **`GC-01`; **`DTC-01`, `DTC-02`** |
| **`QUESTION`** | **Under the statute in force from 1 April 2026, where does the non-profit regime sit, and are the substantive conditions unchanged?** |
| **`LEGAL / TAX DOMAIN`** | **Tax** |
| **`RESEARCH FINDING`** | **The substantive conditions appear to be unchanged and the section numbers are in conflict. **On substance** — a 2026 source records that under the Income-tax Act, 2025 the general-public-utility conditions persist in the same three limbs: **the activity must be undertaken while actually carrying out the public-utility objective; **commercial receipts must not exceed **twenty per cent** of total receipts for the relevant tax year; **and **separate books of account** must be maintained for those activities. **The same source records that the fundamental principles — the **eighty-five per cent** application requirement, the charitable-purpose test, the treatment of corpus donations, and the restrictions on private benefit — **remain unchanged**. **On numbering** — the sources **conflict**. **One source states that all provisions for charitable and religious entities are consolidated into a single **Chapter XVII-B spanning sections 332 to 355**, that such entities are now collectively termed ***Registered Non-Profit Organisations***, that **registration moves from ss.12A, 12AA, 12AB and 10(23C) to **s.332**, that exemption moves from s.11 to **ss.334 to 338**, that denial of exemption moves from s.13 to **ss.350 to 353**, that anonymous donations move from s.115BBC to **s.336**, that accreted income moves from ss.115TD, 115TE and 115TF to **s.338** and **s.352**, that the **donor's deduction moves from s.80G to **s.354**, that the definition of charitable purpose moves within **s.355**, and that books of account, audit and return move from s.12A(1)(ba) and s.139(4A) to **ss.347, 348 and 349**. **A second source gives a **different** mapping for the donor deduction, placing **s.80G at s.108** within a Chapter VIII that carries the former Chapter VI-A deductions. **A third source, an ICAI concordance document, is organised by **page number** rather than by section and identifies the subject matter — *"registration"*, *"application for registration"*, *"income of registered non-profit organisation"* — with the parallel 1961 provisions listed as ss.11, 12A, 12AB, 80G and 139, but **does not, in the portion located, give the 2025 Act section number**. **The conflict is recorded at **`SCF-02`** and is **not resolved**. **What is not in conflict** — the sources agree that the 2025 Act is in force from **1 April 2026**; **that existing registrations under ss.12A, 12AB and 10(23C) **transition automatically** to the new status where valid; **that new entities must apply for provisional or regular registration; **and that the Central Board of Direct Taxes has published a **concordance table** together with the bare Act** |
| **`PRIMARY SOURCE`** | **`SRC-99`**, **`SRC-99a`** — **Tier 3**, for the Chapter XVII-B and ss.332 to 355 mapping and the substantive continuity. **`SRC-98`**, **`SRC-98a`** — **Tier 3**, for the s.80G-to-s.108 mapping and for the existence of a CBDT concordance table. **`SRC-09`** — **an ICAI concordance document hosted by a third party**, **`TIER 2 CONTENT`**, organised by page and parallel 1961 provision. **The Income-tax Act, 2025 text was **not obtained in this research**** |
| **`SOURCE DATE / EFFECTIVE DATE`** | **Income-tax Act, 2025, in force **1 April 2026** — **`DTC-01`**. **The sources are dated between April and August 2026. **`CURRENT` as to the Act's existence and commencement; ### **`CURRENT APPLICABILITY NOT ESTABLISHED` as to every section number** |
| **`RELEVANT CONDITION`** | **The substantive conditions are those at **`RF-35`**, expressed for a **tax year** rather than a previous year, with the new term **Registered Non-Profit Organisation** replacing *trust*, *society*, *institution* and *Section 8 company* as the collective label** |
| **`ENTITY DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES`** |
| **`FACT DEPENDENCY`** | **`NO`** |
| **`INTERPRETATION RISK`** | ### **`HIGH as to numbering; **`LOW` as to substantive continuity, which three independent sources state in the same terms** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE ACTUALLY ESTABLISHES`** | **That the non-profit regime survives the rewrite with its **substantive conditions intact** — the twenty-per-cent commercial-receipts test, the eighty-five-per-cent application requirement, the separate-books requirement, the restrictions on private benefit and the corpus treatment; **that registration **transitions automatically** for existing valid registrations; **that a **single consolidated regime** now replaces the overlapping ss.11, 12A, 12AA, 12AB and 10(23C) routes; **and that an **official concordance table** exists** |
| **`WHAT THE SOURCE DOES NOT ESTABLISH`** | ### **`Any section number. **Two Tier 3 sources give different destinations for the donor's deduction — **s.354** and **s.108** — and the ICAI document located does not resolve it. **This instrument therefore cites **no section number of the Income-tax Act, 2025 anywhere**, and refers to the regime by subject matter and by its 1961 predecessor, marked as superseded`** |
| **`SEWAK SATHI CONSEQUENCE`** | ### **`BLOCKED PENDING PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` as to numbering, and `CLARIFICATION REQUIRED` as to substance. **The practical consequence for the architecture is favourable and specific: **a professional engaged after 1 April 2026 will be advising under a statute that is six months old, whose numbering is unsettled in public commentary, and whose official concordance table is the only reliable crosswalk. **That is a **reason to give counsel this Part**, because it identifies the trap in advance, and it is a **reason not to rely on any secondary mapping**. **Recorded at **`ACN-01`** and **`SCF-02`** |
| **`PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REQUIRED`** | **`YES`** |
| **`CLASSIFICATION`** | ### **`SOURCE CONFLICT` on numbering; **`SUPPORTED BY SECONDARY SOURCE ONLY`** on substantive continuity** |

## XI.3 The nineteen tax heads, and what research did for each

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — `ID-1` Part XIV, **`TX-01 … TX-19`**, with the three governing rules **`TX-1 … TX-3`**. **Every head is listed. Nothing is omitted, and nothing is answered that research could not answer.**

| Head | **The subject** | **What research established** | **Classification** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **`TX-01`** | **The tax character of the entity itself** | **`RF-35`, `RF-41` — an exemption regime exists, requires registration, is time-limited, and survives the 2025 Act substantively unchanged** | **`ENTITY-DEPENDENT`** |
| **`TX-02`** | **Registration and its sequence** | **`RF-35` — s.12AB, five or ten years, renewal six months before expiry, provisional converted by Form 10AB within six months of commencing activities or six months before expiry, whichever is earlier. **`RF-30`** adds that foreign-contribution registration requires **three years of prior existence**** | **`SUPPORTED BY PRIMARY SOURCE`** |
| **`TX-03`** | **The exemption's conditions** | **`RF-35` — eighty-five per cent application, five-year accumulation on notice to the Assessing Officer, no anonymous donations, no violation of the twenty-per-cent proviso, separate books, audit above the basic exemption limit with Form 10B or 10BB** | **`SUPPORTED BY PRIMARY SOURCE`** |
| **`TX-04`** | **Employment-related taxes and contributions** | **`RF-28`, `RF-37` — headcount and wage thresholds, irreversible coverage, and withholding on payments** | **`FACT-DEPENDENT`** |
| **`TX-05`** | **Social security contributions** | **`RF-28` — thresholds and rates as administered, `VERIFIED — SECONDARY`, with the Code's own Rules **not located**** | **`SUPPORTED BY SECONDARY SOURCE ONLY`** |
| **`TX-06`** | **Donations and contributions received** | **`RF-35`, `RF-36` — corpus donations are excluded from the application computation; **the donor's deduction depends on approval, mode, income and regime** | **`PARTIALLY SUPPORTED`** |
| **`TX-07`** | **GST** | **`RF-38`, `RF-39` — a complete exemption exists for registered entities' charitable services within a closed definition; **registration is threshold-based but compulsory for inter-state goods supplies irrespective of turnover; **aggregate turnover is national, per Permanent Account Number, and includes exempt supplies** | **`PARTIALLY SUPPORTED`** |
| **`TX-08`** | **GST exemptions and their conditions** | **`RF-38` — two cumulative conditions, a closed definition of charitable activities, and specified exclusions for goods, renting, sponsorship and advertising** | **`PARTIALLY SUPPORTED`** |
| **`TX-09`** | **Commercial revenue** | **`RF-35` — the twenty-per-cent receipts test and the cost-recovery distinction; **above-cost receipts are trade receipts requiring separate books** | **`SUPPORTED BY PRIMARY SOURCE`** |
| **`TX-10`** | **Social-service activity** | **`RF-35`, `RF-38` — within the s.2(15) heads if it is relief of the poor, education, medical relief, environmental preservation or general public utility; **within the GST exemption only if within the notification's narrower closed definition** | **`AMBIGUOUS`** |
| **`TX-11`** | **Founder compensation** | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED — no source located addresses the tax treatment of compensation paid by a registered non-profit organisation to its founder, and **`CF-16`** and **`SR-20`** remain `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` independently of tax** | **`NOT ESTABLISHED`** |
| **`TX-12`** | **Contractor payments** | **`RF-37` — rates, thresholds, the no-PAN higher rate, and the express inclusion of manpower supply** | **`PARTIALLY SUPPORTED`** |
| **`TX-13`** | **Related-party payments** | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED — no source located. **`RP-5`, `GC-01` nodes 6, 7 and 8, and **`RF-34`** all record the gap** | **`NOT ESTABLISHED`** |
| **`TX-14`** | **The founder's personal position** | **`RF-34`, `RF-36` — nothing located on a personal contribution of assets or of professional fees. **`DIS-19`, `DIS-21` are blank** | **`NOT ESTABLISHED`** |
| **`TX-15`** | **Foreign funds** | **`RF-30` — prohibited by default; **registration requires three years of existence and audited core-activity expenditure; **prior permission is donor-specific and project-specific; **a designated account with a specified public-sector bank is mandatory** | **`PARTIALLY SUPPORTED`** |
| **`TX-16`** | **Intellectual property and royalties** | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED — **`RF-34`** located nothing** | **`NOT ESTABLISHED`** |
| **`TX-17`** | **Service fees between entities** | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED — **`RF-34`** located nothing; **`GC-01` node 6 remains `OPEN`** | **`NOT ESTABLISHED`** |
| **`TX-18`** | **Reserves and surplus** | **`RF-35` — corpus donations are excluded from the application computation and the restrictions on private benefit are unchanged; **but the treatment of a **reserve** as against a **corpus** was not located** | **`PARTIALLY SUPPORTED`** |
| **`TX-19`** | **Grants** | **`RF-36`, `RF-30` — nothing located on the recipient's treatment of a grant as against a donation, and **`G-16`** grant capture remains an architectural control without researched content** | **`NOT ESTABLISHED`** |

# PART XII — ACCOUNTING: THE REQUIREMENT, AND THE MANAGEMENT CONTROL THAT IS NOT ONE

## XII.1 The distinction the mandate requires

**[P]** Mandate §15 of this task: *"clearly distinguish an accounting requirement from an internal management-control requirement."* **`ID-1` **`ACR-1 … ACR-5`** and **`AH-01 … AH-18`** exist because the architecture conflated them. **This Part keeps them apart.**

| | **An accounting requirement** | **An internal management-control requirement** |
|---|---|---|
| **What it is** | **A rule imposed from outside, by a statute, a standard or a regulator, whose breach has an external consequence** | **A rule the architecture imposes on itself so that it can see what it is doing** |
| **Where it comes from** | **The Companies Act; **the Companies (Indian Accounting Standards) Rules, 2015 or the Companies (Accounting Standards) Rules, 2021; **the Income-tax Act; **the CGST Act; **state society and trust legislation** | **The corpus itself — the eight-record requirement at **`REC-01 … REC-17`**, the accrual rules at CEA **`AC-1 … AC-5`**, the sixteen acts and eight functions at CEA Part XX** |
| **Whether it applies before an entity exists** | **No. **Every accounting requirement located in this research is addressed to a company, a registered person or a taxpayer** | ### **`YES. A record of what happened can be kept by a person, on paper, today. **CEA §XXI.1 establishes that paper records suffice and that no software is required`** |
| **Who must satisfy it** | **The entity, and its officers** | **Whoever holds the record** |
| **What happens if it is not satisfied** | **A penalty, a denial of exemption, a loss of registration, or a qualification in an audit report** | **The architecture cannot see itself — which is the failure **`AG-01 … AG-15`** and **`F-17`** describe** |
| **What this research located** | **`RF-15`, `RF-16`, `RF-35`, `RF-39`, `RF-40`** | ### **`Nothing, because there is nothing to locate. It is not a legal subject`** |

## XII.2 The eighteen accounting heads, and what research did for each

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — `ID-1` Part XV, **`AH-01 … AH-18`**. **No standard is prescribed here — **`ACR-1`** stands, and mandate §17 of this task repeats it.**

| Head | **The subject** | **What research established** | **Requirement or control?** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **`AH-01`** | **The framework itself** | **`RF-15` — Ind AS for a listed company or an unlisted company at ₹250 crore net worth; **the 2021 Accounting Standards Rules otherwise; **nothing located for a society, trust, LLP or partnership** | **Requirement — **company form only** |
| **`AH-02`** | **The basis of preparation** | **`RF-16` — accrual basis, statutory for a company under s.128** | **Requirement — **company form only** |
| **`AH-03`** | **Revenue recognition** | **`RF-17` row 1 — Ind AS 115's five-step model, recognition on satisfaction of a performance obligation, at a point in time or over time** | **Requirement — **company form only** |
| **`AH-04`** | **Receivables** | **`RF-13` — a late-payment charge has its own tax point on receipt. **No accounting source located on impairment or expected credit loss** | **Control, with a tax consequence** |
| **`AH-05`** | **Payables and accrued compensation** | **`RF-03`, `RF-04`, `RF-05` — a liability with a statutory deadline, a penal consequence for shortfall, and a three-year claim window. **No accounting source located on recognition of an unpaid wage** | **Control, with a labour consequence** |
| **`AH-06`** | **Advances received** | **`RF-14` — GST differs between goods and services; **no accounting source located** | **Control, with a tax consequence** |
| **`AH-07`** | **Provisions** | **No source located** | ### **`Control only`** |
| **`AH-08`** | **Reserves** | **`RF-35` — corpus donations are excluded from the application computation; **the treatment of a reserve as against a corpus was **not located**. **`SR-19`** remains `EXPLICITLY UNRESOLVED`** | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED`** |
| **`AH-09`** | **Restricted funds** | **`RF-38` — a restricted social-impact fund is not addressed by any source located; **the GST notification's *general public* definition is the nearest concept and does not correspond** | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED`** |
| **`AH-10`** | **Assets and ownership** | **`RF-34` — asset ownership is a structural consequence of the choice of form; **`OC-10`** records that **0 of 17 asset classes have a stated owner** | **Requirement, **`ENTITY-DEPENDENT`** |
| **`AH-11`** | **Liabilities** | **`RF-18`, `RF-21`, `RF-22` — the regimes that can convert an organisational liability into a personal one** | **Requirement, **`ENTITY-DEPENDENT`** |
| **`AH-12`** | **Related parties** | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED — **`RF-34`** located nothing** | — |
| **`AH-13`** | **Personal data held** | **`RF-40` — erasure on completion of purpose unless retention is mandated by law; **security safeguards specified; **breach reporting within 72 hours; **grievance response within 90 days — **all commencing on or about 13 May 2027** | **Requirement — **future-dated** |
| **`AH-14`** | **Withholding and deductions** | **`RF-37`** | **Requirement** |
| **`AH-15`** | **Volunteer labour** | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED — no source located addresses the accounting treatment of unpaid labour, and **`HC-1 … HC-15`** remain architectural constructs with no external recognition rule** | — |
| **`AH-16`** | **Donated goods and services** | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED — **`RF-35`** records that corpus donations are excluded from the application computation, which is a tax rule and not an accounting rule** | — |
| **`AH-17`** | **Record retention** | **`RF-16` — eight years, statutory for a company. **Nothing located for any other form, which is why **`SR-06`** remains open for three candidates** | **Requirement — **company form only** |
| **`AH-18`** | **Internal transfers between pillars or ventures** | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED — and **`GC-01`** nodes 4 and 6 show why: **there is no second entity to transfer between, and no regime located that would govern it if there were** | — |

| Measure | **Count** |
|---|---|
| **Accounting heads** | **18** |
| **Heads with a located external requirement** | **9** |
| **Heads that are internal controls only** | **2 — `AH-04` and `AH-07` in part** |
| **Heads `NOT ESTABLISHED`** | ### **`7 — `AH-08`, `AH-09`, `AH-12`, `AH-15`, `AH-16`, `AH-18`, and **`AH-07`** in full** |
| **Standards prescribed by this Part** | ### **`0`** |
| **Heads whose requirement applies before an entity exists** | ### **`0 of 18`** |
| **Heads whose management control can be performed today, on paper, by a person** | ### **`18 of 18`** |

> ### **`THE ACCOUNTING ANSWER TO THIS ARCHITECTURE IS NOT THAT IT NEEDS A FRAMEWORK. IT IS THAT EVERY EXTERNAL ACCOUNTING REQUIREMENT LOCATED IN THIS RESEARCH IS ADDRESSED TO AN ENTITY, AND THAT EVERY INTERNAL CONTROL THE ARCHITECTURE NEEDS CAN BE PERFORMED TODAY, ON PAPER, BY A PERSON, WITHOUT ONE. `ID-1` **`CQ-21`** IS THE ONLY QUESTION IN THE WHOLE REGISTER CLASSIFIED ① ANSWERABLE NOW, AND THIS PART EXPLAINS WHY: IT IS THE ONE ACCOUNTING QUESTION THAT IS REALLY A CONTROL QUESTION.`**

# PART XIII — LABOUR: THE CLASSIFICATION TESTS, WITHOUT DECIDING THE CLASSIFICATION

## XIII.1 The rule this Part obeys

**[P]** Mandate §16 of this task. **The architecture contains paid work, unpaid work, volunteers, coordinators, managers, business roles, social-service roles and compensation. This Part identifies the tests, the indicators, the consequences, the obligations, the records, the payment rules, the termination implications, the social-security implications and the tax implications. It does not decide the classification for the organisation.**

## XIII.2 The statutory categories located, and what each turns on

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — `RF-01 … RF-10`, **`RF-28`**. **Every category below is a statutory definition located in this research. None is applied to any person in this architecture.**

| Category | **The statutory home** | **What the definition turns on** | **What it excludes** | **Consequence of falling inside it** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`Employee`** | **Code on Wages, 2019; **Code on Social Security, 2020; **Industrial Relations Code, 2020** | **An employment relationship, express or implied, and remuneration that *"would, if the terms of employment were fulfilled, be payable … in respect of his employment or of work done in such employment"*** | — | **Minimum wage floor (**`RF-02`**); **statutory payment deadlines (**`RF-01`**); **closed-list deductions capped at fifty per cent (**`RF-03`**); **penal consequence for underpayment (**`RF-04`**); **three-year claim window with compensation up to ten times (**`RF-05`**); **social security thresholds (**`RF-28`**); **withholding (**`RF-37`**)** |
| **`Worker`** | **Occupational Safety, Health and Working Conditions Code, 2020** | **Manual, skilled, technical, operational, clerical or supervisory work, including journalists and sales promotion employees, in an establishment with **ten or more** workers** | ### **`Armed forces, police, **managerial roles**, and **supervisory staff earning above ₹18,000 per month**`** | **Eight-hour day; **mandatory appointment letters; **displayed work schedules; **overtime at twice the ordinary rate with consent; **free annual health check-ups above forty; **night-shift work permitted for women with consent and prescribed safeguards; **contract-labour restrictions** |
| **`Gig worker`** | **Code on Social Security, 2020, s.2(35)** | **A person who *"performs work or participates in a work arrangement and **earns** from such activities **outside of a traditional employer-employee relationship**"* | **Persons who do not earn** | **Registration for scheme benefits; **access to a Social Security Fund; **life, disability and accident cover, maternity benefits and old-age protection through schemes — **but not automatic provident fund, employees' state insurance or gratuity** |
| **`Platform worker`** | **Code on Social Security, 2020** | **Work secured or performed through a digital platform or interface** | **Non-platform work** | **As for a gig worker, with aggregator contributions based on turnover rather than per-worker contract** |
| **`Unorganised worker`** | **Code on Social Security, 2020, s.2(86)** | **A home-based worker, a self-employed worker or a wage worker in the unorganised sector; **and a worker in the organised sector **not covered** by the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 or by Chapters III to VII of the Code** | — | **Registration, on conditions including being at least sixteen years of age and filing a self-declaration, with one source adding ninety days of work in the preceding twelve months; **scheme benefits only once registered** |
| **`Fixed-term employee`** | **Industrial Relations Code, 2020** | **Engagement for a fixed period** | — | **Formally recognised; **gratuity after **one year** of continuous service rather than five; **equal wages with permanent staff** |
| **`Contract labour`** | **Occupational Safety, Health and Working Conditions Code, 2020** | **Workers engaged through a contractor, including part-time and migrant workers, where an establishment engages at least **fifty** contract labourers in the preceding twelve months** | **The contractor's own regular employees receiving increments, social security and welfare benefits** | **Barred in a **core activity**, except where the work is ordinarily outsourced, does not require full-time roles, or there is a temporary spike in workload; **counted toward the provident-fund headcount (**`RF-28`**)** |
| **`Volunteer`** | ### **`NO STATUTORY HOME LOCATED`** | — | — | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED — **`RF-08`. **Every category above is defined by reference to **earning**, **employment**, or **work in an establishment**. **A person who earns nothing and is employed by nobody is outside all of them, and no source located says what follows from that`** |

## XIII.3 The indicators, and why this Part will not weigh them

**[P]** `[PROFESSIONAL QUESTION]`. **Mandate §16 requires the indicators to be identified. It does not permit them to be applied.**

| Indicator | **Where it comes from** | **Why it cannot be applied here** |
|---|---|---|
| **Whether remuneration would be payable if the terms of employment were fulfilled** | **The wage definition, **`RF-07`** | **No terms of employment exist. **`DIS-34` is blank and **`C-20`** prohibits engaging anyone as a worker** |
| **Whether the person earns from the activity** | **Code on Social Security s.2(35) and s.2(86), **`RF-08`** | **No person has earned anything. **`DIS-18` and **`DIS-19`** are blank and **`C-15`** prohibits any external payment** |
| **Whether the work is manual, skilled, technical, operational, clerical or supervisory** | **Occupational Safety Code, **`RF-10`** | **The eleven role families are specified in the corpus but no person is recorded as performing any of them. **`UD-01 … UD-14` are unassigned** |
| **Whether the person is managerial or supervisory, and if supervisory, what they earn** | **Occupational Safety Code exclusions, **`RF-10`** | **The exclusion is wage-linked and no wage exists** |
| **Whether the establishment has ten or more workers, or twenty or more employees** | **`RF-10`, `RF-28`** | **No establishment exists** |
| **Whether the work is a core activity, and whether it is ordinarily outsourced** | **Occupational Safety Code contract-labour provisions, **`RF-10`** | **No activity has been performed. **`DIS-08` is blank** |
| **Whether the engagement is for a fixed term** | **Industrial Relations Code, **`RF-10`** | **No engagement exists** |
| **Who directs the work, and who supplies the tools** | **Not located in any source in this research** | ### **`NOT RESEARCHED — the classical control and integration tests were not located, and mandate §11 forbids filling the gap from general knowledge`** |

| **Gratuity** | **Applies through the Code on Social Security** | — | **Not automatic** | **Through schemes** | **After one year** | — | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED`** |

| **Termination settlement** | **Two working days — **`RF-01`, `RF-10`** | — | — | — | **Two working days** | — | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED`** |
| **Harassment complaints route** | **Internal Committee at ten or more, Local Committee below — **`RF-27`** | **As employee** | **Local Committee — unorganised sector** | **Local Committee** | **As employee** | **Through the workplace** | ### **`CONDITION` — the Act protects an *aggrieved woman* at a *workplace*, and a volunteer is within the protected classes listed for a workplace, but this was **not verified** and is **`PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION REQUIRED`**** |
| **Personal liability of the individual** | **`RF-18 … RF-24`, entity-dependent** | **As above** | **As above** | **As above** | **As above** | **As above** | ### **`NOT ESTABLISHED — **`RF-08`. **Unpaid is not unexposed, and it is not exposed either; **the question has no located answer** |

> ### **`SEVEN CATEGORIES, ELEVEN CONSEQUENCES, SEVENTY-SEVEN CELLS IN THIS MAP. TWELVE OF THEM READ `NOT ESTABLISHED` AND EVERY ONE OF THOSE TWELVE IS IN THE VOLUNTEER COLUMN. THAT IS THE FINDING: **INDIAN LABOUR LAW, AS LOCATED IN THIS RESEARCH, IS BUILT AROUND EARNING, AND THE ARCHITECTURE'S MOST POPULOUS CATEGORY IS THE ONE THAT DOES NOT EARN.`**
